## Semantic Chunks
### Section: Introduction
- **Level**: `intro`
- **Hash**: `70a4aaf6c6d6871235753092d4ce152c7a9fcc26d7cb605f54ad0682d2138812`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/can-writing-history-show-whether-a-student-used-ai` (Similarity: 0.9321)

```text
Draft history can give teachers something an AI detector alone cannot: evidence about how an assignment developed over time.

Instead of reviewing only the final essay, a teacher may be able to see whether a student built the assignment gradually, revised ideas over several sessions, added large sections of text at once, replaced rough writing with much more polished passages, or pasted a nearly complete draft into the document.

These patterns help teachers investigate possible ChatGPT or other AI use. But draft history should not be treated as automatic proof: a large block of text appearing at once could come from AI, or it could come from the student's own draft written in another document.

The best approach is to use draft history to identify specific questions , then compare those observations with AI detection, previous student writing, sources, plagiarism results, and the student's explanation.

Checkmark Plagiarism's essay writing playback helps teachers examine the writing process alongside AI detection , static AI detection, plagiarism detection , autograding , and integrations with Canvas and Google Classroom .
```

### Section: What Is Draft History?
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `a1ac702b8dce1d23bfcb8ee96636ed295ec8ef5eb9c1958161713ea2241e65f1`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/what-does-suspicious-writing-history-look-like` (Similarity: 0.8483)

```text
Draft history records how a document changed during creation: when text appeared, what was deleted, how paragraphs moved, and when sources were added. While the submitted paper answers "what did the student submit?" draft history helps answer: "How did the student get from a blank page to this final assignment?"
```

### Section: 1. Look for Gradual Drafting
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `5d19ee84e2963ce7418c07d5dd7f0a88638f9f72b9314addea0181784a992b92`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/how-can-i-tell-the-difference-between-ai-writing-and-a-student-improving` (Similarity: 0.8637)

```text
Gradual drafting (outlines, rough drafts, rewritten sentences, reorganized arguments) indicates a substantial multi-session writing process. However, do not assume gradual drafting guarantees zero AI assistance, as students may use AI for brainstorming or sentence-level suggestions.
```

### Section: 2. Look for Large Additions of Text
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `324f63193f559f39c586e99342e77178a484671f3efd7694df5dc943df26e6c8`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/how-can-i-compare-a-students-assignment-to-their-previous-writing` (Similarity: 0.8520)

```text
When 700+ words appear in a single event, ask: "Where did you write this section before adding it here?" The draft may have originated from Microsoft Word, another Google Doc, research notes, or generative AI.
```

### Section: 3. Look at Activity After a Large Addition
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `38cffee8d5d11816ba87b865672d6d20a2311d4ad05d579d38cc1dac21d9a1c1`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/what-does-suspicious-writing-history-look-like` (Similarity: 0.8135)

```text
Compare whether an inserted block undergoes substantive multi-paragraph revisions or only cosmetic comma and typo edits. Minimal subsequent editing suggests the text was finalized elsewhere.
```

### Section: 4. Look for Abrupt Changes in Writing Quality
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `967ce04afeab0e9167071bd1ab0d9586b14d2dad91926e3e976dfced4cd309f2`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/what-does-suspicious-writing-history-look-like` (Similarity: 0.8241)

```text
When an essay transitions from informal, error-prone sentences to complex academic clauses with flawless punctuation, draft history helps identify whether that stylistic shift coincided with an isolated text insertion.
```

### Section: 5. Look for Paragraph Replacements
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `b684a851d710ffa6a7e1da302ac503b16eb948b75af510e3d234a359d5959783`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/do-real-student-essays-usually-have-deletions-and-revisions` (Similarity: 0.7854)

```text
A rough student paragraph suddenly swapped for sophisticated academic prose warrants inquiry: "How did you revise this paragraph from the earlier version to this one?"
```

### Section: 6. Look at How Sources Enter the Draft
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `b5a484d91ef3f9545b47c8be3bbd05ef3cbe49b1bff8374d248b436f3479596f`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/what-does-suspicious-writing-history-look-like` (Similarity: 0.9141)

```text
Verify whether citations and quotes appeared gradually alongside student analysis or arrived as a complete multi-paragraph block. Hallucinated or non-existent citations provide clear objective evidence of generative AI involvement.
```

### Section: Draft History vs. AI Detection vs. Plagiarism Detection
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `7bc0f237a851f4f50801b58f17c6dacf3cbfdb54ca55c3a9602c1bd26e54853a`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/what-does-suspicious-writing-history-look-like` (Similarity: 0.9026)

```text
AI Detection (The Product)

Analyzes statistical language patterns (perplexity, burstiness) in the final submitted text.

Draft Playback (The Process)

Examines timeline changes, keystrokes, revision cycles, and text insertion events over time.
```

### Section: What Does a Concerning Pattern Look Like?
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `aaac4e431f3cbdf04d0eba681834a51d1f4e14946885f999a4771a034ff06699`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/what-does-suspicious-writing-history-look-like` (Similarity: 0.9694)

```text
Concerning Corroborating Pattern

1,300 words appear in one instant block 
 Near-zero subsequent revisions 
 Writing style diverges completely from past work 
 AI detection raises severe flags on pasted text 
 Several citations do not exist in databases 
 Student cannot explain arguments or source origins

Valid External Drafting Pattern

1,200 words pasted from external Word doc 
 Student provides earlier Word draft with multi-session logs 
 Student fluently explains thesis, terms, & citations 
 Citations are fully verified in academic databases 
 Explanation completely resolves the initial paste question
```

### Section: A Practical Draft-History Investigation Process
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `a8644b8d1bf1bdb9e49ab84e5f929ff23ec6f3737c2736f17ec5c3837b9b3324`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/can-writing-history-show-whether-a-student-used-ai` (Similarity: 0.9066)

```text
A Practical 15-Step Draft-History Investigation Process:

1. Read the finished assignment submission thoroughly first. 
 2. Identify what specific textual or conceptual elements raised initial concern. 
 3. Review how the draft developed across the document timeline. 
 4. Note large additions, major paragraph replacements, or abrupt style changes. 
 5. Examine what editing occurred immediately before and after those events. 
 6. Compare the submission against previous student writing baselines. 
 7. Review AI detection reports as one corroborating signal among several. 
 8. Check plagiarism detection results for traditional source matches. 
 9. Verify suspicious sources, quotes, and citations in academic databases. 
 10. Ask the student neutrally to explain unusual drafting activity. 
 11. Ask the student to explain the content, thesis, and reasoning of questionable sections. 
 12. Give the student an opportunity to provide earlier draft files or version logs. 
 13. Inquire neutrally about generative AI tools, writing assistants, and tutoring support. 
 14. Compare the student's explanation with the observable draft history. 
 15. Apply course AI policy and school academic-integrity procedures if concerns persist.
```

### Section: Can teachers use draft history to detect ChatGPT?

- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/can-writing-history-show-whether-a-student-used-ai` (Similarity: 0.8826)

```text
Draft history reveals writing patterns that justify further investigation, such as large polished text additions or sudden rewrites. It usually cannot prove by itself that ChatGPT was the source.
```

### Section: What should teachers look for in draft history?
- **Level**: `h3`
- **Hash**: `983bfdbe3cd28ef2d2ec303d82decf3b4f15208ce9c4fdaf87e47660d10aec3a`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/what-should-i-look-for-when-a-students-writing-suddenly-improves` (Similarity: 0.8146)

```text
Look for large text additions, abrupt changes in style, substantial replacements, minimal revision after polished content appears, and activity that conflicts with the student's explanation.
```

### Section: Does a large paste mean a student used AI?
- **Level**: `h3`
- **Hash**: `83d01835a8648e43e9c87490aafd43fa793f779829f4916258e5c6da6fd914b1`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/can-writing-history-show-whether-a-student-used-ai` (Similarity: 0.9493)

```text
No. The student may have drafted elsewhere in Word or notes and moved their own writing between documents.
```

### Section: Can gradual drafting prove that the student did not use AI?
- **Level**: `h3`
- **Hash**: `751f1d13f6abf0c81c83eaadcc547b707fb5f41c980d60443270a5bfecea5a29`

